[17/09/2026, 21:53:04] ~ Adeyinka Medus: The METI UniPods AI Innovation Programme - Cohort 1 Chatbot Hackathon is a challenge to solve a real problem we all face in the group. It is like a side hustle. Not a major reason why we are here. 

The Problem:
Chat volume is too high; people miss messages and ask the same questions again. People also miss meetings and can't rewatch all the recordings, so information gets lost.

The Challenge:
Build a working AI chatbot that can read chats + call recordings and answer people's questions directly.

How to go about it:
1. Form a team TODAY:
- Up to 5 people
- Members cannot all be from the same country
- Must include at least one woman
- Deadline: Close of business TODAY, Thursday 17 Sept 2026

To declare your team, the Team Lead should send an email with:
Subject: `UniPods Hackathon`
Body: Team Name + Names of members + their countries

2. Build Phase:
- Friday 18 Sept to Thursday 24 Sept
- You must submit: A working chatbot + source code link + short setup notes on how to run it.

3. Prize & Judging:
- $5,000 cash prize
- Your solution will actually be deployed to solve the problem
- Judging will be done by the whole group.

It's not a slide deck competition - they want a working bot that solves the FAQ/catchup problem.

Do you have a team already? If not, you should quickly join an incomplete team here.

[18/09/2026, 07:52:30] ~ meti_bot: 🌙 *Daily Recap — 17 September*

The day was mostly about the *hackathon*: lots of energy went into forming teams before the deadline (introductions, finding teammates, balancing gender and nationalities). Several teams came together.

📚 *Wadhwani session (Module 1 — Problem Statement)*
Charles had everyone work on their problem statement: start with *who* experiences the problem, *what* it is and the *root cause* — never the solution. Keep it to one short paragraph (350 characters, then 150 to sharpen it). Separate your *primary* and *secondary* customer, and think about *who will pay*. Personal written feedback comes at *Milestone 1* (~3 weeks). The recording is available.

🎥 *UN video-demo opportunity*
- A *5-minute* slot to present your solution at the UN General Assembly event ("Building the Workforce of the Future", 20 Sep, New York).
- *Deadline: Friday 18 Sep, 2:00 PM CAT.* Video *5 min max* (shorter is fine).

Bonjour, je suis UniConnect-BOT, développé par l'équipe UniConnect.

J'ai lu tout ce qui s'est dit dans ce groupe, ainsi que le brief du hackathon et le guide vidéo. Deux façons de me parler :

- Dans le groupe : écris @ask puis ta question. Je ne réponds que si on m’appelle, jamais autrement.
- En privé : écris moi directement, sans @ask, et personne d’autre ne le voit. C’est là que je sers le plus.

Voici ce que je sais de ce groupe.

- - -

What this group is

This is the WhatsApp group for Cohort 1 of the UNDP timbuktoo UniPods METI AI Innovation Programme. It brings together 300+ selected innovators from across Africa (chosen from 670 applicants across 24 countries) who are doing AI and entrepreneurship training together. It is run by UNDP staff who post session links, deadlines and documents here.

What has been happening

- The programme has four tracks: MIT Universal AI (self-paced online course), Wadhwani Ignite (14-week entrepreneurship programme with live sessions), later advanced training with the Ethiopian AI Institute, and an in-person bootcamp in Addis Ababa for selected teams.
- MIT course invitations went out by email around 15 September. Only the person whose email applied is enrolled, and the certificate is in that person's name. Team mates cannot be added; the enrolled person may share their login. Many people had trouble finding the course: use the exact enrolment link from the email, then click Dashboard, not Home.
- Wadhwani Ignite started 15 September. Early confusion about an email calling it "Ignite" was resolved: Wadhwani's own system is also called Ignite, so the emails were correct. Charles Bolton runs those sessions; there is a separate Wadhwani WhatsApp group, link sent by email.
- A chatbot hackathon was launched, and it has dominated the group. It came out of complaints that the chat is too busy and people miss things. Gift proposed it, Diane published the guidelines. $5,000 prize, one winning team, judged by a vote of the whole group.
- Enormous team-formation activity: people posting skills, countries and gender, because each team needs at least one woman and cannot be all from one country. A group poll (41 to 5) allowed two members from the same country.
- Bots being tested one team per day in the group, scheduled by Diane. Shadrak's bot (meti_bot / PodPal) went first on 18-19 September and answered hundreds of questions; Nexus was next. Other teams named include AskBack AI Bot, Swift Agents, Wise-Bot.
- A UN demo video call: one innovator's 5-minute video to be shown at a UNGA side event in New York. Guidelines were shared as a document.
- A team/business-name Google Sheet had to be reissued after someone edited the headers.
- Side threads: several smaller WhatsApp groups by sector (health, agritech, edtech), a UniPods TikTok account, and an optional "Pitch Arena" group.

Dates that matter

- 8 September (past): first call / Q&A.
- 10 September (past): Welcome and Orientation session.
- 15 September (past): programme start; Wadhwani Module 1; MIT invitation emails arrived.
- 16 September (past): MIT Universal AI welcome and onboarding call.
- 16 September (past): deadline to fill in your row on the team/business sheet was Wednesday 16 September.
- 17 September (past): hackathon team declarations due, close of business, by email.
- 18 September, 2:00 PM CAT (past): UN demo video submission deadline.
- 18 September (past): first METI Open Hour "Ask Us Anything".
- 18 to 24 September: the hackathon runs. Submission deadline Thursday 24 September.
- 20 September (past): the UNGA "Building the Workforce of the Future" event in New York.
- 18 October 2026: expected completion date for the MIT Universal AI course to stay eligible.
- Week of 23 November 2026: Ethiopian AI Institute selects the 50 strongest projects.
- 1 December 2026: Addis Ababa bootcamp begins, one person per selected team.
- February 2027: second bootcamp for teams not selected.
- Recurring: Wadhwani live sessions Tuesdays and Thursdays; Open Hours Mondays with Gift and Wednesdays with Diane.

Who does what

- Diane: main programme coordinator and admin. Posts links, sheets, guidelines, adds members, schedules bot testing. Ask her by DM or by email.
- Gift Ntuli: programme lead. Ran the first call, proposed the hackathon, answers policy questions.
- Munira: MIT Universal AI contact.
- Jeovaire Umukundwa: part of the UNDP support team.
- Charles Bolton: Wadhwani Ignite facilitator, runs the platform sessions.
- Shadrak (Benin): built the first working hackathon bot, deployed in the group.

Things you keep needing

- Programme email for everything: unipods.regional@undp.org
- MIT Universal AI support: uaisupport@mit.edu
- MIT course page: https://learn.mit.edu/universal-learning/ai
- Wadhwani Ignite signup: https://web.nen.wfglobal.org/en/login?mode=createAccount&source=student
- Team/business name sheet: https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/15sAD53FA9LZXJ7EzOIzWLALTViaPz2_e/edit
- Wadhwani session Teams link: https://teams.microsoft.com/meet/419860837373470?p=jYchWkDZnC4etsclnK (ID 419 860 837 373 470, passcode g2Z7gc7Q)
- MIT session Teams link: https://teams.microsoft.com/meet/369123389215172?p=E4YbRFyzKjPR5AFYUf
- Open Hour Teams link: https://teams.microsoft.com/l/meetup-join/19%3ameeting_MjlkNWYyMjYtMGNhMi00NDM1LTlkNmYtOTZhYTU2MDU4MDc2%40thread.v2/0?context=%7B%22Tid%22%3A%22b3e5db5e-2944-4837-99f5-7488ace54319%22%2C%22Oid%22%3A%2225f213f2-0e2f-4763-83fa-0d909a0e9701%22%7D
- Recordings: MIT onboarding https://drive.google.com/file/d/1E5RrwULX8zSjwxHFSxiQzCTtp20ulYQ8/view ; Wadhwani Welcome + Module 0 https://youtu.be/yVji4ZQECVw ; Module 1 class https://youtu.be/6q4uPBO_sDc ; Module 1 coaching/Q&A https://youtu.be/-6G7LXiu47o

Still open

- How judging of the hackathon will actually work with many bots live at once. More info was promised and never posted. Testing slots were still being booked as late as 28 September, past the stated 24 September deadline.
- Whether late team declarations, formed after 17 September, will be accepted.
- Whether a team may have three members from the same country. The document says only that members cannot all be from the same country; the two-per-country line came from a group poll, not an admin ruling.
- Whether the UN video deadline was extended for people with upload problems. Asked, never answered.
- Whether every team member must complete the courses or only the enrolled founder.
- Details of the UniPod end-of-month meeting and transport expenses. Asked, never answered.
- Most country UniPods had not contacted participants as of 18 September; Gift said contact would come the following week.
- Several people complained the group is too fast for French speakers and that information is being missed.

- - -

From tomorrow I will post five lines each morning on what changed, and nothing else. Demain matin je posterai cinq lignes sur ce qui a changé, et rien de plus.

[21/09/2026, 13:31:27] ~ Shinzii: privacy
[21/09/2026, 13:31:27] ~ Abdulmajid Haruna Saeed: Privacy
[21/09/2026, 13:31:29] ~ Jymns Bot: *Privacy*

- I store messages of this group (text, captions, voice-note transcripts) in the programme's own database so I can answer questions and write summaries. Raw messages are kept for 365 days.
- From them I keep short Q&A notes (e.g. "When is demo day? - Friday") and dated calendar items (sessions, deadlines, announcements). Admins can remove any of them (*faq remove*, *event remove*, *wrong*).
- Messages deleted "for everyone" are deleted from my memory too.
- Private chats with me are not stored; I only remember the last few turns for 30 minutes.
- I only message you privately after you have written to me first. I note that you did, and keep answers waiting for you (for up to 7 days) until you do.
- Text is sent to the configured AI provider to generate answers. I never share one group's content with people outside that group.

*PROGRAMME*

- *today* — sessions, deadlines and news for today
- *upcoming* — next sessions, trainings and UniPod meetups
- *deadlines* — every date ahead, with days remaining
- *announcements* — newest first (*announcements 30* = last 30 days)
- *tracks* — where MIT, Wadhwani, the Institute and the bootcamp stand

*CATCHING UP*

- *catchup* — what you missed since your last message
- *summary* or *summary 12* — last 24 hours, or last 12
- You can also just ask: "I need a recap of this week
  sessions", "can you summarize today?"

  post minutes. *meetings* lists them.

- *faq add <question> || <answer>* · *faq remove <number>*
- *wrong* — reply to an answer of mine to make me forget it
- *autoanswer on* / *off* · *digest set <hours>* · *digest off*
- *delete* — reply to a message
- *announce <text>* — post a programme announcement
- *import* — send a chat export .txt with this caption 'import' to
  load history from before I joined

Reply to any unanswered question I forward you.

Bonjour, je suis UniConnect-BOT, développé par l'équipe UniConnect.

J'ai lu tout ce qui s'est dit dans ce groupe, ainsi que le brief du hackathon et le guide vidéo. Deux façons de me parler :

- Dans le groupe : écris @ask puis ta question. Je ne réponds que si on m’appelle, jamais autrement.
- En privé : écris moi directement, sans @ask, et personne d’autre ne le voit. C’est là que je sers le plus.

Voici ce que je sais de ce groupe.

- - -

What this group is

This is the WhatsApp group for Cohort 1 of the UNDP timbuktoo UniPods METI AI Innovation Programme. It brings together 300+ selected innovators from across Africa (chosen from 670 applicants across 24 countries) who are doing AI and entrepreneurship training together. It is run by UNDP staff who post session links, deadlines and documents here.

What has been happening

- The programme has four tracks: MIT Universal AI (self-paced online course), Wadhwani Ignite (14-week entrepreneurship programme with live sessions), later advanced training with the Ethiopian AI Institute, and an in-person bootcamp in Addis Ababa for selected teams.
- MIT course invitations went out by email around 15 September. Only the person whose email applied is enrolled, and the certificate is in that person's name. Team mates cannot be added; the enrolled person may share their login. Many people had trouble finding the course: use the exact enrolment link from the email, then click Dashboard, not Home.
- Wadhwani Ignite started 15 September. Early confusion about an email calling it "Ignite" was resolved: Wadhwani's own system is also called Ignite, so the emails were correct. Charles Bolton runs those sessions; there is a separate Wadhwani WhatsApp group, link sent by email.
- A chatbot hackathon was launched, and it has dominated the group. It came out of complaints that the chat is too busy and people miss things. Gift proposed it, Diane published the guidelines. $5,000 prize, one winning team, judged by a vote of the whole group.
- Enormous team-formation activity: people posting skills, countries and gender, because each team needs at least one woman and cannot be all from one country. A group poll (41 to 5) allowed two members from the same country.
- Bots being tested one team per day in the group, scheduled by Diane. Shadrak's bot (meti_bot / PodPal) went first on 18-19 September and answered hundreds of questions; Nexus was next. Other teams named include AskBack AI Bot, Swift Agents, Wise-Bot.
- A UN demo video call: one innovator's 5-minute video to be shown at a UNGA side event in New York. Guidelines were shared as a document.
- A team/business-name Google Sheet had to be reissued after someone edited the headers.
- Side threads: several smaller WhatsApp groups by sector (health, agritech, edtech), a UniPods TikTok account, and an optional "Pitch Arena" group.

Dates that matter

- 8 September (past): first call / Q&A.
- 10 September (past): Welcome and Orientation session.
- 15 September (past): programme start; Wadhwani Module 1; MIT invitation emails arrived.
- 16 September (past): MIT Universal AI welcome and onboarding call.
- 16 September (past): deadline to fill in your row on the team/business sheet was Wednesday 16 September.
- 17 September (past): hackathon team declarations due, close of business, by email.
- 18 September, 2:00 PM CAT (past): UN demo video submission deadline.
- 18 September (past): first METI Open Hour "Ask Us Anything".
- 18 to 24 September: the hackathon runs. Submission deadline Thursday 24 September.
- 20 September (past): the UNGA "Building the Workforce of the Future" event in New York.
- 18 October 2026: expected completion date for the MIT Universal AI course to stay eligible.
- Week of 23 November 2026: Ethiopian AI Institute selects the 50 strongest projects.
- 1 December 2026: Addis Ababa bootcamp begins, one person per selected team.
- February 2027: second bootcamp for teams not selected.
- Recurring: Wadhwani live sessions Tuesdays and Thursdays; Open Hours Mondays with Gift and Wednesdays with Diane.

Who does what

- Diane: main programme coordinator and admin. Posts links, sheets, guidelines, adds members, schedules bot testing. Ask her by DM or by email.
- Gift Ntuli: programme lead. Ran the first call, proposed the hackathon, answers policy questions.
- Munira: MIT Universal AI contact.
- Jeovaire Umukundwa: part of the UNDP support team.
- Charles Bolton: Wadhwani Ignite facilitator, runs the platform sessions.
- Shadrak (Benin): built the first working hackathon bot, deployed in the group.

Things you keep needing

Still open

- How judging of the hackathon will actually work with many bots live at once. More info was promised and never posted. Testing slots were still being booked as late as 28 September, past the stated 24 September deadline.
- Whether late team declarations, formed after 17 September, will be accepted.
- Whether a team may have three members from the same country. The document says only that members cannot all be from the same country; the two-per-country line came from a group poll, not an admin ruling.
- Whether the UN video deadline was extended for people with upload problems. Asked, never answered.
- Whether every team member must complete the courses or only the enrolled founder.
- Details of the UniPod end-of-month meeting and transport expenses. Asked, never answered.
- Most country UniPods had not contacted participants as of 18 September; Gift said contact would come the following week.
- Several people complained the group is too fast for French speakers and that information is being missed.

*Programme and learning*
- The MIT Universal AI course is self-paced.
- Expected completion date: *18 October 2026*.
- MIT course:

https://aii.et/

- Nexus is the UniPods METI AI programme assistant bot.

*Hackathon*
- The challenge is to build a *practical, working chatbot* that solves a real communication problem.
- A slide deck, idea, or mock-up alone is not enough.
- The chatbot may be delivered through:
- WhatsApp
- Telegram
- A web interface
- There is no single required platform.
- The submission must include:
- A working chatbot
- Access to the source code or repository
- Short setup instructions
- Bots should be named using the team name followed by *“BOT”*.
- Teams must inform *Diane* before deploying a bot. Bots should not be deployed randomly.
- Testing slots are booked up to *3 October*. Testers act as judges and record scores while testing the bots.
- Hackathon testing and project work run through *24 September 2026*.
- The submission deadline is *Thursday, 24 September 2026*.
- The separate 5-minute UN video-demo deadline on 18 September at 2:00 PM CAT has already passed.

*Team rules*
- A team can have a maximum of *5 members*.
- Each team must include *at least one woman*.
- Team members should come from different countries.
- The reported rule allows up to *2 members from the same country*.
- A solo founder can participate by writing *“Solo”* in the team sheet.
- Team declarations were due at the end of business on *17 September 2026*.
- Late declarations may still be emailed, but confirmation should be obtained from Diane.
- To declare a team, email *unipods.regional@undp.org*.
- Use the subject: *UniPods Hackathon
- Team Declaration*.
- Include the team name, all members, and their countries.
- There is no separate declaration link.

*Registration*
- Complete only your solution’s row in the registration sheet.
- Add the business name and team members.
- For a solo founder, write *“Solo”*.
- Registration sheet:
https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/15sAD53FA9LZXJ7EzOIzWLALTViaPz2_e/edit

*Selection of the 50 projects*
- The 50 projects are selected from teams that:
- Complete the MIT Universal AI course
- Stay up to date with the Wadhwani programme
- Selected teams work with the Ethiopian AI Institute to create a tailored workplan.
- In the last week of November 2026, the Institute selects the 50 strongest solutions for the Addis Ababa bootcamp.
- Each selected team sends one participant.
- Teams not selected for the November bootcamp may be considered for the second bootcamp in *February 2027*.

*Programme dates and sessions*
- The broader UniPods Hackathon week is listed as *17–24 September 2026*.
- The chatbot hackathon itself is listed as *18–24 September 2026*.

[22/09/2026, 18:35:32] ~ UniConnect-BOT: Because that is the hackathon problem statement: the group grew large, high chat traffic means people miss messages and re-ask questions already addressed, people miss meetings and cannot rewatch call recordings, so information gets lost across chats and calls. The challenge is to build a chatbot that makes sense of all that data, calls, chats and more, and answers people directly.

One caveat raised in the group: a bot that picks up random unnecessary conversations and stores them is unfit, because the programme team would pay a lot of API cost for irrelevant information in the knowledge base.

[22/09/2026, 22:14:34] ~ UniConnect-BOT: Here is what the group messages contain:

The hackathon problem statement
- The group has grown large and it is hard to keep up with every message.
- High chat traffic means people miss messages and re-ask questions already answered.
- People miss meetings and cannot rewatch call recordings, so information gets lost across chats and calls.

Bot testing
- Diane is pushing to finish all bot testing this week; if your bot is ready, tell her and start testing.
- JYMNS Bot's test day was 22 Sep 2026. You can also ask Jymns Bot about the programme in DM.
- Nexus Bot is back with DM/inbox chat available and slight improvements.
- Slots were booked until Sat 26, with a request that only one person per group books.

Debate about the solutions
- Some feel the hackathon has drifted from a WhatsApp chatbot into a standalone platform, and question whether MCP integration (Claude, VS Code) is needed.
- Complaints that setting up an MCP agent is too complex and the bot should live where the discussion already happens, and that users just want chats distilled and summarised.
- Ecosync's reply: code is open source, contributions and security suggestions welcome, the access code exists so only participants get access, and the aim is a personalised agent you chat with without opening WhatsApp.

Upcoming event
- METI AI Innovation Programme Cohort 1 Needs Assessment Workshop with the Ethiopian AI Institute team, Wednesday 23 September, 10:00-11:30 CAT/East Africa Time, on what support you need (compute, mentorship, testing, access to institutions).
- Join: https://teams.microsoft.com/l/meetup-join/19%3ameeting_MjgxNmY4NGItZTZlMi00OTNmLTk2YzEtMjg0ZTdmYWJjM2Q4%40thread.v2/0?context=%7b%22Tid%22%3a%22b3e5db5e-2944-4837-99f5-7488ace54319%22%2c%22Oid%22%3a%2225f213f2-0e2f-4763-83fa-0d909a0e9701%22%7d

On the programme length, members discussed that the 3 months likely cover the whole programme and physical presence in Ethiopia may be shorter, but this was unconfirmed.

Not in these messages: prizes, submission requirements and deadlines.

[22/09/2026, 22:19:11] ~ UniConnect-BOT: What this group is

This is the WhatsApp group for Cohort 1 of the UniPods METI AI Innovation Programme 2026, run by UNDP / timbuktoo UniPods with METI. It holds 300+ founders, students and AI builders from across Africa, selected from 670 applicants in 24 countries. Everything here revolves around the programme: onboarding calls, the MIT Universal AI course, the Wadhwani Ignite entrepreneurship track, and a chatbot hackathon the group launched itself.

What has been happening

Introductions and country/startup roll call (4 to 5 Sep). Hundreds of members posted names, countries, ventures and LinkedIn links. You do not need to read these.

Onboarding and programme structure. Diane and Gift NTULI ran welcome and orientation calls, shared the info pack and the slide deck, and confirmed the programme is 14 weeks with later in-person training in Addis Ababa. Programme start date was 15 September.

MIT Universal AI course. Invitation emails went out 15 Sep. Only the email address that applied was invited; teammates cannot get their own invite, though Gift and Munira said you may share your login (the certificate stays in the applicant's name). Self-paced, no instructor. Munira is the contact for MIT questions.

Wadhwani Ignite platform. A separate 14-week track with live sessions, run with Charles Bolton in a separate Wadhwani WhatsApp group. An email naming "Wadhwani IGNITE" caused a panic on 8 Sep; Gift confirmed it was correct, the Wadhwani system is simply also called Ignite.

Team and business details sheet. Members fill in only their own row, Business Name and Team Members columns. Teams capped at 5 and cannot be changed. Solo founders write "Solo".

The chatbot hackathon. Gift joked that someone should build a bot to answer repeated questions; the group turned it into a real hackathon with a $5,000 prize, judged by the whole group. Teams up to 5, not all from one country, at least one woman. A large part of the recent chat is people forming teams.

Bot testing. Diane runs a rota, one bot at a time, each team gets a day, then disconnects. Bots seen so far: meti_bot (Shadrak), Nexus Bot, JYMNS Bot, UniConnect, EcoSync, with AskBack, PodPal and others queued. Diane wants all testing closed by Friday.

Debate about what the bots should be. Strong feedback that the solution must live inside WhatsApp and simply summarise the chat, not become a separate platform requiring logins or MCP setup. EcoSync defended its approach as a personalised agent; most testers pushed back.

UN General Assembly demo video. One innovator's 5-minute demo was to be shown at "Building the Workforce of the Future" in New York on 20 September. Guidelines were circulated as a document.

Language. French speakers repeatedly said they were falling behind. Gift confirmed METI 2026 is in English (2025 was in French). Shadrak later shared French transcripts of the session videos.

Recommendation letters. Gift announced on 21 Sep that UNDP will issue certificates on completion instead of recommendation letters; country UniPods will help connect you to partners you identify.

Side groups. Sector groups for health, agritech, edtech, plus a Pitch Arena group and the UniPods TikTok.

Dates that matter

8 September, welcome and onboarding call (past)
10 September, Welcome and Orientation session, 3pm CAT (past)
15 September, programme start (past)
16 September, deadline to fill in the team/business sheet; MIT welcome call (past)

25 September, Diane's stated cut-off for finishing all bot testing
30 September, next Open Hour / group meeting (Diane, Wednesday 30th)
Note: a bot stated 18 October as the MIT course completion date. That came from a bot, not from an organiser.

Who does what

Diane, main day to day admin. Meeting links, sheets, adding teammates, bot testing schedule, letters. Send her things by inbox.

Still open

How the hackathon judging will actually be scored. "More info on judging to follow" was promised; Diane told people to note scores themselves, but no scoring sheet was ever shared.
Whether the $5,000 goes only to the winner or could be split with runners-up. Suggested, never answered.
Late team registration requests after the 17 September deadline. Asked several times, no clear answer in the group.
Whether server or compute resources will be provided to deploy the winning bot. Asked, unanswered.
Transport and accommodation costs for in-country UniPod meetings. Asked twice, unanswered.
Several MIT platform faults, blank course pages and "invite link expired", never resolved in the group.
Full parity of information for French speakers. Raised repeatedly and still not solved by the organisers.

Traduction en français de votre message

L'énoncé du problème du hackathon
- Le groupe est devenu très grand et il est difficile de suivre chaque message.
- Le volume élevé de messages fait que les gens en manquent et reposent des questions déjà traitées.
- Les gens manquent les réunions et ne peuvent pas revoir les enregistrements des appels, donc l'information se perd entre les discussions et les appels.

Les tests des bots
- Diane pousse pour terminer tous les tests de bots cette semaine ; si votre bot est prêt, dites-le lui et commencez à tester.
- Le jour de test de JYMNS Bot était le 22 septembre 2026. Vous pouvez aussi poser des questions sur le programme à Jymns Bot en message privé.
- Nexus Bot est de retour, avec la discussion en message privé disponible et de légères améliorations.
- Des créneaux étaient réservés jusqu'au samedi 26, avec la demande qu'une seule personne par groupe réserve.

Le débat sur les solutions
- Certains estiment que le hackathon s'est éloigné d'un chatbot WhatsApp pour devenir une plateforme autonome, et se demandent si l'intégration MCP (Claude, VS Code) est nécessaire.
- Des plaintes indiquent que configurer un agent MCP est trop complexe, que le bot devrait se trouver là où la discussion a déjà lieu, et que les utilisateurs veulent simplement que les échanges soient résumés et synthétisés.
- Réponse d'EcoSync : le code est open source, les contributions et suggestions de sécurité sont les bienvenues, le code d'accès existe pour que seuls les participants y accèdent, et l'objectif est un agent personnalisé avec lequel discuter sans ouvrir WhatsApp.

Événement à venir
- Atelier d'évaluation des besoins du Programme d'innovation METI AI, Cohorte 1, avec l'équipe de l'Institut éthiopien de l'IA, le mercredi 23 septembre, de 10h00 à 11h30 CAT / heure d'Afrique de l'Est, sur le soutien dont vous avez besoin (ressources techniques ou puissance de calcul, mentorat, appui aux tests, accès aux institutions, ou autres ressources). Lien Teams partagé par Diane dans le groupe.
[22/09/2026, 22:19:32] ~ UniConnect-BOT: A note on language: you asked for a translation into any language of my choice, so I have written this in English.

What this group is

This is the WhatsApp group for Cohort 1 of the UniPods METI AI Innovation Programme 2026, run by UNDP with timbuktoo UniPods and METI. It holds around 300 or more selected innovators and founders from across Africa, plus programme staff, and it is used for announcements, meeting links, course support and now a hackathon. Selection was from 670 applicants across 24 countries, and the 2026 cohort runs in English (2025 was in French).

What has been happening

Onboarding and introductions. The group opened on 4 September with hundreds of self introductions, countries, startups and LinkedIn links. Diane pinned the welcome message.

The programme info pack. Gift NTULI shared "UniPods METI AI Program 2026 Cohort 1 Info pack.pdf" on 5 September. Gift confirmed there will be access to capital funding, and that team members may join the group.

MIT Universal AI course. Invitation emails from MIT Learn began arriving 15 September. Only the email address that applied received an invite. Gift and Munira confirmed you may let a teammate use your login, but the certificate stays in the name of the invited person. The course is self paced, no instructor. Common problems: blank pages, missing course on the dashboard, expired invite links.

Wadhwani Ignite entrepreneurship programme. A 14 week track with live classes and coaching sessions, run via Microsoft Teams, with Charles Bolton. There is a separate Wadhwani WhatsApp group for platform problems; Gift directs technical questions there. Some people were confused by emails naming "Wadhwani IGNITE"; Gift confirmed this is correct, the Wadhwani system is also called Ignite.

The team and business name spreadsheet. Diane asked everyone to fill only their own row, Business Name and Team Members. Solo founders write "Solo". Teams are capped at 5 and cannot be changed. If you registered solo, teammates added later were not considered at interview.

The UN General Assembly demo video. One innovator gets a 5 minute pre recorded slot at "Building the Workforce of the Future", GABI Unstoppable Africa, New York. Guidelines were circulated by Gift and Diane.

The chatbot hackathon. Born in the group on 16 September when Gift joked that someone should build a bot to answer repeated questions; Victor Akpan volunteered and it became a formal challenge with a 5,000 dollar prize. Teams formed loudly in the chat for two days.

Bot testing and judging. Each team gets a day to connect its bot to the group so everyone can test it. Diane manages the rota and asks teams to disconnect when their day ends. Bots so far: meti_bot (Shadrak, first out), Nexus Bot, JYMNS Bot, UniConnect Bot (use @ask), AskBack Bot, EcoSync, PodPal BOT. Diane asked everyone to keep their own scores, because the group is the judge, and not to vote for themselves.

Debate about what the bots should be. Several members pushed back on solutions that move people off WhatsApp to a separate platform or require MCP setup in Claude or VS Code. The repeated user message: we want the chats distilled and summarised where the conversation already happens. EcoSync replied that their code is open source, feedback and security suggestions are welcome, the access code is there so only participants get in, and their aim is a personalised agent you use without opening WhatsApp.

The French and English gap. gnplus and others said repeatedly that francophone members are missing information. Munira and others translate ad hoc; Shadrak later shared French transcriptions of session videos.

Country UniPods. Gift asked who had heard from their national UniPod; most countries had not. He said they were notified and contact should come the following week. UNDP decided that completers get certificates rather than recommendation letters; needs should be raised so the in country UniPod can connect you to partners.

Dates that matter

4 September 2026 (past): group opened, introductions.
5 September 2026 (past): info pack shared.
8 September 2026 (past): first welcome and onboarding call.
10 September 2026 (past): Welcome and Orientation session, 15:00 CAT / 16:00 EAT / 14:00 WAT.

On the summary you were sent

The points in that message are accurate as far as they go: the hackathon problem statement is quoted correctly from the brief, Diane did push to close testing by Friday, JYMNS Bot tested on 22 September, Nexus Bot returned with DM support, and the EcoSync debate happened as described. Second, the team rule in the document is that members cannot all be from the same country, plus at least one woman and a maximum of five people; the group poll suggesting "two from the same country is fine" was a chat discussion, not the written rule.

[23/09/2026, 12:46:46] ~ Shadrak bsh: Salut ! Bien sûr, je peux te faire un récapitulatif du meeting d'aujourd'hui. 😊

Il y a eu l'atelier "Needs Assessment Workshop" qui s'est terminé il y a peu, à 9h30 CAT.

Cet atelier a abordé l'importance d'évaluer la santé d'une startup à travers un exercice appelé "Viral Assessment". C'est une analyse des écarts sur neuf métriques clés, qui aide à comprendre où se situe la startup et quels sont les points à améliorer pour progresser. Les métriques incluent l'équipe fondatrice, la proposition de valeur, le marché, le modèle économique, l'acquisition de clients, l'adéquation produit-marché, la mise à l'échelle et la stratégie de sortie. L'idée est d'identifier le maillon faible pour mieux concentrer ses efforts.

On a aussi parlé de la plateforme Abaca, qui peut aider dans cette démarche d'évaluation. Le processus de construction d'une plateforme peut prendre environ deux semaines, et une page d'atterrissage peut être utilisée pour présenter ses fonctionnalités et offrir des réductions. Après la finalisation, il est conseillé de contacter les clients par SMS ou WhatsApp pour leur annoncer que le produit est prêt.

[23/09/2026, 15:28:24] ~ Joy Ujunwa: @⁨Zak Bot⁩ give me a summary of today's Needs Assessment Workshop ‎<This message was edited>
[23/09/2026, 15:34:17] Zak Bot: Yes @⁨~Joy Ujunwa⁩, Yes. Here is a summary of the Needs Assessment workshop on Wednesday 23 September 2026 (METI AI Innovation Programme, with the Ethiopian AI Institute team). Facilitator: Abineder.

The session was about knowing where your startup really is, what is blocking the next step, and what to focus on next.
Main idea Diagnose before you prescribe. Many founders think they need more funding, marketing, or staff. Often the real gap is customer validation, proof people will pay, retention, or stronger product validation. Decide from evidence, not assumptions.

Five stages
Discovery – real problem, clear target customer
Validation – solution works and customers value it
Traction – demand and customers willing to pay
Growth – repeat acquire and retain
Scaling – systems and processes to expand sustainably

Age, headcount, and funding raised do not define the stage. Evidence does (interviews, payments, repeat buyers, documented processes, SOPs, expansion plans, etc.).
AI startups Keep testing. Problem, customer, solution, and demand can shift fast. Test assumptions, experiment, learn, pivot, iterate.
VIRAL assessment (Village Capital)

 Tool to spot gaps across: founding team, vision, value proposition, investable market, business/revenue model, customer acquisition beyond early adopters, product-market fit, scaling, exit readiness.

For each area, mark the highest milestone you can back with real evidence (documents, customer touchpoints, agreements, transactions, etc.). Then look at the pattern: the weakest critical area is what should be your immediate priority, not fixing everything at once.

[23/09/2026, 23:34:19] Zak Bot: @⁨~gnplus⁩, d’après la capture, tu es bien sur *MIT Universal AI*. Ce n’est pas forcément cassé :

1. *View Certificate* = module déjà fini
2. *Continue* = tu es en cours (Python Part 1, Supervised and Unsupervised Learning)
3. *Start* = pas encore commencé (Foundations of Neural Networks)

Les *16 modules fondation* sont obligatoires. Les *verticals* sont optionnels. Date visée : *18 octobre 2026*.

Si le souci c’est une *page blanche* quand tu cliques Continue / Start :

1. Ouvre le cours depuis *Dashboard* (pas Home)
2. Utilise le *lien d’invitation MIT Learn* de ton e-mail, pas le site générique
3. Onglet *Feedback* à droite, ou *uaisupport@mit.edu*

Dis-moi si c’est la page blanche, un module qui ne s’ouvre pas, ou autre chose, et je t’oriente plus précisément 🙂

[24/09/2026, 00:52:58] Zak Bot: 🚀 New Update: Meet the Upgraded UniPods AI Assistant, Zak v1.1

Hello METI AI Innovators and Builders! 👋

As fellow members of this cohort, we experience many of the same challenges you do.

Important meeting links can disappear under hundreds of messages. Slide decks and useful resources become difficult to find. Members may miss sessions and struggle to catch up. Even opening the assistant repeatedly through a browser link can become inconvenient.

These are not just hackathon problem statements to us. They are real challenges affecting how we learn, collaborate, and make the most of the UniPods experience.
That is why we have continued listening to your feedback and improving Zak.

Zak began as our hackathon solution, but our commitment goes beyond the competition. We want to keep building, learning from the community, and adapting the platform around the problems you actually face.

🌟 What’s New in Zak v1.1

📅 1. Live Meetings and Masterclass Hub
Never lose an important session link again.
Visit Meetings to find upcoming meeting links, session agendas, mentor information, and recorded recaps, all organised in one place.
Zak also provides GMT, WAT, CAT, and EAT timezone helpers so members across different countries can follow every session more easily.

📂 2. One-Stop Resources Centre
Official cohort folders, AI toolkits, handbooks, pitch deck templates, learning materials, and other useful resources are now available under Resources.
You can search by topic or tag and open the material you need without searching through hundreds of chat messages.

📱 3. Install Zak as an App
Zak is now a Progressive Web App, which means you can install it directly on your phone or computer.
• No app store download required
• Opens in full-screen mode like a regular app
• Provides faster access from your home screen
• Works across supported mobile and desktop browsers
Tap “Install app” in the top bar, or select “Add to Home Screen” from your browser menu.

💡 4. Request a Feature
We want the community to help shape what Zak becomes.
Use the 💡 Request feature button in the sidebar to tell us about a tool, resource, integration, or improvement you would like Zak to support.
Your requests will help us understand what to build next and will be shared with the appropriate programme coordinators.

⚡️ 5. Slash Commands and Easier Navigation
Type / in the chat to access quick commands such as:
/meetings
/resources
/ask
/help

Zak also has a redesigned sidebar and Dark and Light modes for a smoother experience across mobile and desktop.

🎯 Try the New Zak

🌐 Open Zak: https://zak-app.xerotek.io
📱 Install it from the top-right corner or add it to your home screen.
📅 Visit Meetings to check upcoming sessions.
📂 Open Resources to find important cohort materials.
💬 Ask Zak a question and let us know how well the answer helps you.

🚀 This Is Only the Beginning

We are building Zak alongside the UniPods community because we are part of this community too.

The hackathon gave us the opportunity to begin solving these problems, but our work will not stop when the competition ends. We are committed to improving the platform, correcting what does not work, adding useful integrations, and responding to the changing needs of the cohort.

Your feedback, feature requests, and everyday use of Zak will shape what it becomes.

More importantly, please keep testing it and telling us what would make it genuinely useful to you.

Thank you for your energy, feedback, creativity, and commitment to building Africa’s AI future.

Keep learning. Keep collaborating. Keep building. 🌍💡

The Zak Team
Fellow UniPods Cohort Members and Hackathon Participants

Jeli can:
* Catch you up on conversations you missed
* Reconstruct the history of discussions & decisions
* Summarize & transcribe our meetings & calls

- Can send reminders for our MIT sessions in the our DMs & in the group
- Can take calls
- Reads & responds in multiple languages English, French, Swahili etc
* Responds in voice notes for accessibility to visually impaired members
* Translates documents shared in different languages

I'm *Jeli*, the griot of this group. Just as the griots of old kept their community's memory, I keep yours: the announcements, the decisions, the recorded sessions, the deadlines. Nothing gets lost.

*What you can ask me*
• _"When is the next session?"_ — I answer, and I say who announced it
• _"What did I miss since Monday?"_ — I'll catch you up
• _"Summarise yesterday's session"_ — I listen to the recordings
• _"Send me the Information Pack"_ — I'll send the document, translated if you ask
• _"Remind me before the meeting"_ — I'll tell you in time, here or privately
• _/deadlines_ — everything due in the next two weeks

Write to me in English, en français, kwa Kiswahili, በአማርኛ — I answer in your language. In writing or by voice, whichever suits you: I can listen to a voice note and reply with one, in Swahili, Amharic, Hausa, Yoruba, Kinyarwanda and others. You can even *call me and talk to me out loud* — ask me for "the call link" and I'll send it 📞

*Two things that matter.* I only speak when spoken to: start your message with "Jeli", or reply to one of mine. And I never say anything nobody said here — when I don't know, I say so.

Good luck to every team 🙏

[24/09/2026, 14:42:04] ~ Akpan, Victor D. : @⁨‪+234 903 450 0579‬⁩ what is the meti program about?
[24/09/2026, 14:42:28] ‪+234 903 450 0579‬: Hey Victor! 👋

The *METI UniPods AI Innovation Programme* is an AI skilling + venture-building programme funded by Japan's Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry (METI), run by UNDP.

*Here's what it does:*

- Builds a pipeline into *timbuktoo*, UNDP's pan-African startup support initiative (early-stage capital, policy support, technical support via 10 thematic hubs)
- Works through *UniPods* — makerspaces in public universities across Africa that are one-stop shops from ideation to prototyping, testing, and market access (currently 23 UniPods in 21 countries, with 5 more expected by December 2026)
- Cohort 1 started with *250 solutions* selected from 670 applicants across 24 countries

*The programme has four main components:*

- MIT Foundational AI (self-paced, covers intro to AI, machine learning, Python, data analysis, sector applications)
- Wadhwani Entrepreneurship & Innovation (14-week instructor-led track on business model, market, fundraising)
- Ethiopian AI Institute (intermediate/advanced AI, virtual + in-person bootcamp in Addis Ababa)
- In-person bootcamp in Addis Ababa (December 2026 for top 50 teams)

*Plus:* non-equity work-plan grants pre-bootcamp, and cash grants + equity model post-bootcamp.

Any specific questions about the tracks or timeline?
[24/09/2026, 14:42:33] ~ Macs: The METI UniPods AI Innovation Programme is an AI skilling + venture-building programme funded by Japan's Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry (METI), run by UNDP. It aims to build a pipeline into timbuktoo (UNDP's startup support), operates through UniPods makerspaces, and involves MIT Foundational AI, Wadhwani Entrepreneurship & Innovation, and Ethiopian AI Institute tracks, culminating in an in-person bootcamp in Addis Ababa for top teams in December 2026. It also offers grants.

📝 Summary
• Maria Segala from MIT Open Learning gave an overview of the Universal AI program, which consists of 16 foundational modules and optional industry-specific vertical modules.
• Participants need to achieve a 60% score primarily through assignments and knowledge check questions to obtain a certificate for a module.
• Due to a limited number of licenses, teams must choose one designated member to take the MIT course using their personalized registration link.
• Learners have 12 months of access to the platform to review materials and access certificates.
• Gift Ntulí announced upcoming country unipot open pod events and office hours to support participants.

✅ Decisions
• Teams must use the specific email address they applied with and assign one person to complete the MIT Universal AI course due to limited licenses.
• The first country unipot open pod event should take place before the end of the month, with transport and accommodation covered for future events from October onwards.
• Office hours will be held on Mondays and Wednesdays, with an additional session this Friday.

📋 To do
• Reach out to country unipots to organize open pod events before the month ends — all teams
• Complete the Universal AI foundational modules and assignments to achieve at least 60% — designated team members
• Check email for the verification link and create a new password if experiencing login errors — Kasama
• Email Diane if the MIT course enrollment email was not received — Marc Ka

[24/09/2026, 19:51:15] ~ aether.silver: 👋 Good everyone everyone!
Before you dive in, quick intro to Sentinel — our submission for the UniPods Chatbot Hackathon.
The problem: this group moves fast. People miss messages, ask questions that were already answered, or miss calls and can't rewatch them. Info gets lost.
Sentinel fixes that. It reads the group's messages, calls, and shared files, and answers you directly — grounded in what was actually said, with sources, not guesses.
What it can do:
✅ Answers from real messages (with sources)
✅ Catches you up on what you missed
✅ Sends back PDFs shared in the group
✅ Reads voice notes
✅ Pulls an admin's last messages — @sentinel what did Diane last say?
✅ Shows who's most active — /stats in DM
✅ Lists the admins — @sentinel who are the admins?
✅ Can DM you a private answer — @sentinel DM me that
✅ Speaks major languages — English, French, Arabic, Hausa, Nigerian Pidgin, Yoruba, Sesotho, and more
✅ Says "I don't know" instead of guessing
✅ DMs stay private — never leaked back to the group
How to test it:
Mention it: @sentinel <your question>
Try: @sentinel what did I miss?
Try: @sentinel who are the admins?
Try: @sentinel what did [name] last say?
Want a private answer? @sentinel DM me that
Try a different language: @sentinel (ask in Arabic, French, or your own)
In DM only: /stats
Go ahead and try it out — ask it anything, via text or voice note, see what it does. We'd love your honest feedback! 🚀

[24/09/2026, 20:49:44] ~ sentinel: METI AI Program vs MIT Course vs Wadhwani Program vs Hackathon  

* *METI AI Program* – the overall cohort‑1 initiative that runs the whole 14‑week journey, including coursework, coaching, and the Wadhwani entrepreneurship track.  
* *MIT Course* – a self‑paced “Emerging Talent” module that every participant must complete within a month; it’s the learning backbone that the METI program builds on.  
* *Wadhwani Program* – the 14‑week entrepreneurship track that runs concurrently with the METI program; teams register at least one member to finish it and receive coaching, Q&A, and Ignite content.  
* *Hackathon* – a separate, one‑off event (not the chatbot‑challenge) that teams can enter to showcase solutions; it’s optional and distinct from the regular METI/Wadhwani schedule.  

*Sources*: [1] admin reminder of MIT session, [2] info pack detailing METI, MIT, and Wadhwani structure, [3] member note on hackathon.

[24/09/2026, 21:11:04] ~ aether.silver: @sentinel
[24/09/2026, 21:11:10] ~ sentinel: 👋 What can I help with?
[24/09/2026, 21:11:12] ~ sentinel: Crack the MIT Universal AI course in a day by treating it like a sprint:

* Set a clear goal — finish the 16 mandatory modules (Python, ML, DL, GenAI, prescriptive AI) — you’ll need to power through every video/reading.  
* Chunk it: break the day into 4‑hour blocks, each covering 4 modules; take 10‑minute breaks to reset.  
* Use the “optional weekly office hour” for quick Q&A on anything you’re stuck on (see the info pack).  
* Stay focused: mute notifications, work in a quiet spot, and keep a notebook for key formulas or code snippets.  
* Test as you go: run the provided notebooks after each module to lock in the concepts.  

It’s intense, but the course is self‑paced and meant to be finished within a month, so a 24‑hour sprint is doable if you stay disciplined.  

— Sentinel

[24/09/2026, 21:18:38] ~ sentinel: AI is the broad umbrella — the idea of machines that can act “intelligent”.  
ML is a subset of AI that teaches computers to learn patterns from data without being explicitly programmed.  
DL is a deeper layer of ML that uses multi‑layer neural networks to automatically extract high‑level features from raw data.

*MIT’s foundational AI course first covers AI concepts, then a dedicated machine‑learning module, and finally a deep‑learning module.*
